### General non-avian dinosaur research

- Review of preceding studies and of methods used to study dinosaur biology and evolution is published by [Xu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xu_Xing_\(paleontologist\)) et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-85]

- Henderson (2026) determines rates of discovery of new dinosaur taxa and changes geographical origin of new discoveries throughout the history of the study of the group.

  [^cite_note-86]

- Brownstein & Griffin (2026) reconstruct early evolutionary history of dinosaurs, placing the emergence of the group between 251.2 and 230 million years ago, and reporting evidence of a burst of rapid morphological evolution in the Middle to early Late Triassic that coincided with emergence of major dinosaurian subgroups (Ornithischia, Sauropodomorpha and Theropoda).

  [^cite_note-87]

- [Aureliano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tito_Aureliano) et al. (2026) compare the microstructure of appendicular bones in non-avian dinosaurs and large-bodied mammals, and interpret it as indicating that gigantism was achieved through divergent evolutionary pathways in the two groups.

  [^cite_note-88]

- Wilson et al. (2026) study the evolution of the body size of Mesozoic dinosaurs, finding no evidence of a correlation between body size and climatic conditions before or after shifts in preferred climatic niches.

  [^cite_note-89]

- Saitta (2026) argues that models of growth in dinosaurs used in studies from preceding years may be underfit, failing to account for possible causes variation between specimens other than ontogeny.

  [^cite_note-90]

- Evidence of limited range of motion of fingers of *[Camarasaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camarasaurus)* and *[Stegosaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stegosaurus)* is presented by Senter (2026).

  [^cite_note-91]

- Evidence from the study of extant birds and crocodylians, interpreted as indicating that [gastrolith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gastrolith) shape is a reliable proxy for diet and stomach muscularity in extinct dinosaurs, is presented by Takasaki et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-92]

- Forster et al. (2026) provide evidence from the study of fossils from coastal and interior upland ecosystems preserved in Cretaceous formations from North America interpreted as indicating that elevated δ

  13

  C values of skeletal materials of dinosaurs and other vertebrates from their ecosystems might be related to consumption of marine-derived organic matter (including marine macroalgae and plants) by dinosaurs and other animals from coastal environments.

  [^cite_note-93]

- Regalado Fernández (2026) develops a function that enables estimates of phylogenetic informativeness of fossil specimens and its relation to skeletal completeness, and applies it to the fossil record of dinosaurs from Mexico.

  [^cite_note-94]

- Review of factors influencing the formation of dinosaur tracks is published by Falkingham & Gatesy (2026).

  [^cite_note-95]

- Hartmann et al. (2026) provide a method for recognizing patterns of shape variation differentiating dinosaur tracks with the use of unsupervised machine learning, and use it to study affinities of controversial dinosaur tracks, reporting evidence of small, three-toed, bird-like footprints from the Triassic and Early Jurassic falling within the bird-dominated region of morphospace, and evidence of some Middle Jurassic tridactyl tracks from the Isle of Skye (Scotland, United Kingdom) grouping with ornithopods rather than with theropods.

  [^cite_note-96]

- Ha & Kim (2026) present a new automated system for detection of dinosaur tracks across diverse tracksites and preservation conditions.

  [^cite_note-97]

- Peng & Xing (2026) review the utility of new analytical tools for reevaluation of long-standing hypotheses in dinosaur ichnology, and argue that not all tridactyl tracks can be assigned to either theropod or ornithopod trackmakers solely on the basis of footprint shape, that parallel trackways increase the probability of group movement of sauropods but do not concusively prove it, and that Triassic and Early Jurassic tracks with bird-like morphology do not conclusively prove an early origin of birds.

  [^cite_note-98]

- Granata et al. (2026) report evidence of impact of erosion on preservation of dinosaur tracks from the Carnian strata of the Lerici ichnosite in Italy (the type locality of *[Evazoum sirigui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evazoum?action=edit\&redlink=1)*).

  [^cite_note-99]

- Licht et al. (2026) provide a new temporal framework for dinosaur assemblages from the Khorat Plateau (Thailand), reporting evidence of a [Pliensbachian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pliensbachian) age of the [Nam Phong Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nam_Phong_Formation), Kimmeridgian to early Berriasian age of the [Phu Kradung Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phu_Kradung_Formation), Hauterivian–Barremian age of the [Sao Khua Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sao_Khua_Formation) and the Aptian–Albian age of the [Phu Phan Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phu_Phan_Formation) and the [Khok Kruat Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khok_Kruat_Formation).

  [^cite_note-100]

- Ait Haddou et al. (2026) report the discovery of new tracksites preserving theropod and quadrupedal dinosaur (possibly stegosaur or sauropod) tracks from the strata of the Jurassic [Tilougguit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tilougguit_Formation) and [Guettioua formations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guettioua_Formation) (Morocco).

  [^cite_note-101]

- Evidence indicating that theropod and ornithopod tracks from the Middle Jurassic strata in the Tizi N'Talghemt area (Morocco) were originally left on [tidal flats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tidal_flat) during low tide points is presented by El Ouali et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-102]

- DePolo et al. (2026) study the dinosaur tracks from the Middle Jurassic sauropod-dominated Duntulm tracksite from the Isle of Skye (Scotland, United Kingdom), finding no definitive evidence of gregarious behavior of sauropods from the study, and revise the fossil record of dinosaur tracks from the Isle of Skye, reporting possible evidence of different environmental preferences of sauropods and other dinosaurs.

  [^cite_note-103]

- Tracks of sauropods, probable ankylosaurs and undetermined bipedal dinosaurs preserved in aeolian depositional setting are described from the Hauterivian strata of the [Agrio Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agrio_Formation) (Argentina) by Heredia et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-104]

- Ornithischian and theropod (possibly including large dromaeosaurid) tracks are described from the Lower Cretaceous strata of the [Serra do Tucano Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serra_do_Tucano_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) (Brazil) by Barros et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-105]

- A new assemblage of ornithopod (representing two distinct ichnotaxa), sauropod and theropod tracks is described from the Aptian-Albian strata of the [Matoushan Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matoushan_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) at the Huashiban tracksite (Yunnan, China) by Xu et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-106]

- An ornithopod-dominated track assemblage, also including ankylosaurid and theropod tracks, is described from the Coniacian strata of the [Marshybank Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marshybank_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) (British Columbia, Canada) by Plint & Helm (2026).

  [^cite_note-107]

- Choi et al. (2026) identify dinosaur eggs assigned to at least two distinct oogenera (*[Macroelongatoolithus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macroelongatoolithus)* and *[Mosaicoolithus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mosaicoolithus)*) from the Cenomanian strata of the [Dadaepo Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dadaepo_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) (South Korea).

  [^cite_note-108]

- Evidence indicating that dinosaur eggs from the Upper Cretaceous strata of the Wido Volcanics (Wi Island, South Korea) assigned to the ootaxon *[Propagoolithus widoensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Propagoolithus?action=edit\&redlink=1)* were laid in nests established before the igneous intrusion rather than in a rock that was already metamorphosed as a result of volcanic activity is presented by Kim et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-109]

- Cardozo et al. (2026) report the discovery of [faveoloolithid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faveoloolithidae) eggshells from the late Maastrichtian strata of the [Lago Colhué Huapí Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lago_Colhu%C3%A9_Huap%C3%AD_Formation) (Argentina), originating from strata with abundant hadrosaurid remains and no sauropod remains reported to date, and expanding known geographic distribution of Faveoloolithidae in South America.

  [^cite_note-110]

- Pantelides et al. (2026) present a new mechanistic energetic model for the studies of the capability of terrestrial vertebrates for transoceanic dispersal, apply it to extant vertebrates, *[Lambeosaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lambeosaurus)* and *[Rapetosaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapetosaurus)*, and interpret their findings as suggestive of feasibility of dispersal of hadrosaurs and titanosaurs between Africa and Europe through the oceanic corridor separating Iberia from Morocco during the Cretaceous.

  [^cite_note-111]

- Evidence of [Albian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albian) age of dinosaur-rich [Candeleros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candeleros_Formation) and [Huincul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huincul_Formation) formations (Argentina) is presented by Maniel et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-112]

- Dai et al. (2026) study the stratigraphy and palaeoenvironmental features of the strata of the [Zhengyang Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhengyang_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) (Chongqing, China) preserving fossils of Late Cretaceous dinosaurs, interpreted as living in a restricted intermountain basin.

  [^cite_note-113]

- Roberts et al. (2026) study the stratigraphy and age of the strata of the [Hell Creek Formation](3551034.md) from the excavation site of the "[Dueling Dinosaurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dueling_Dinosaurs)", from other locations on the Murray Ranch and from the McGinnis Butte section (Montana, United States), and determine the age of the "Dueling Dinosaurs" locality to be approximately 66.895 million years.

  [^cite_note-114]

- Wyenberg-Henzler & Scannella (2026) study a skull of *[Edmontosaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmontosaurus)* from the Hell Creek Formation (Montana, United States) with a tyrannosaurid tooth embedded in the nasal around the time of death of the hadrosaurid, interpreted as likely resulting from a bite to the snout of *Edmontosaurus* during a predation attempt.

  [^cite_note-115]

- Han et al. (2026) study the hydrodynamic evolution of the Shanyang Basin (China) during the latest Cretaceous, reporting evidence of reduction of suitable nesting habitats of dinosaurs before the [Cretaceous–Paleogene extinction event](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cretaceous%E2%80%93Paleogene_extinction_event).

  [^cite_note-116]

- A study on the fossil record of non-mammalian vertebrates from the Cretaceous-Paleogene transition of the [Ferris Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferris_Formation) (Wyoming, United States) is published by Wroblewski (2026), who places the local Cretaceous-Paleogene boundary 50 m above the previous proposed location, and reports evidence of decrease of recorded diversity of dinosaurs during the latest Maastrichtian that might have either taphonomical or biological causes.

  [^cite_note-117]

### Saurischian research

- Pawlak et al. (2026) identify [lungfish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lungfish) [aestivation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aestivation) burrows in the Triassic strata of the [Ørsted Dal Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%98rsted_Dal_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) (Greenland), interpreted as indicative of a seasonally dry climate in the studied area during the late [Norian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norian), and indicating that aridity was not a barrier for dispersal of theropods and sauropodomorphs living in the studied area at the time.

  [^cite_note-118]

- Menkem et al. (2026) study the sedimentary setting of sauropod and theropod tracks (including a footprint of a large-bodied theropod) from the Lower Cretaceous (Berriasian–Hauterivian) strata of the Koum Basin (Cameroon), and report evidence of shared ichnological and sedimentological traits with tracksites of similar age from northeastern Brazil, interpreted as consistent with existence of a dispersal corridor linking West Africa and South America before the full opening of the Atlantic Ocean.

  [^cite_note-119]

- A new assemblage of sauropod and theropod tracks is described from the Lower Cretaceous (Barremian–Aptian) strata of the [Shinekhudag Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shinekhudag_Formation) (Mongolia) by Mainbayar et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-120]

#### Theropod research

- Boisvert et al. (2026) compare the median size classes of ceratosaurians, megalosauroids, allosauroids, megaraptorans, and tyrannosauroids throughout the Jurassic and Cretaceous periods, and report evidence of a higher median number of missing size classes in tyrannosauroid-dominated ecosystems compared to allosauroid/megalosauroid-dominated ones.

  [^cite_note-121]

- A study on the skull biomechanics and likely feeding behaviors of members of diverse theropod subgroups, with a focus on [tyrannosauroids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyrannosauroidea), is published by Johnson-Ransom et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-122]

- Hendrickx (2026) explores the evolution of the dentition in non-[coelurosaur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coelurosauria) theropods (e.g.,[Ceratosauria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceratosauria), [Megalosauroidea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megalosauroidea), and [Allosauroidea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allosauroidea)).

  [^cite_note-123]

- Evidence from the study of teeth of *Allosaurus fragilis*, *Ceratosaurus dentisulcatus*, *Irritator challengeri* and *Tyrannosaurus rex*, indicating that tooth position is one of the factors affecting microwear texture in theropod teeth, is presented by Morrison et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-124]

- Pereyra (2026) compares the evolution of maxillary shape and size in medium-sized and large abelisaurids, carcharodontosaurs and coelurosaurs, reporting evidence of different adaptive responses to ecological and environmental pressures in the studied groups.

  [^cite_note-125]

- Scherer, Steell & Upchurch (2026) report evidence of different patterns of forelimb reduction in at least five distinct theropod lineages, as well as evidence of correlation of forelimb reduction with cranial robusticity and gigantism (itself likely linked to the increased size of prey animals), and interpret forelimb reduction in theropods as likely linked to redundancy of forelimbs in prey capture.

  [^cite_note-126]

- Evidence from elliptic [Fourier analysis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourier_analysis) of [pedal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pes_\(anatomy\)) [phalanx bones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phalanx_bone) of North American tyrannosaurids, ornithomimids, caenagnathids and troodontids, indicating that theropod pedal phalanges can be identified down to the family level on the basis of the study of their morphology, is presented by Warnock-Juteau, Smith & Cullen (2026).

  [^cite_note-127]

- Drzewiecki et al. (2026) interpret theropod tracks from the Lower Jurassic [East Berlin Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Berlin_Formation) (Connecticut, United States) as produced in an area with an ephemeral lake system rather than at the margin of a perennial lake.

  [^cite_note-128]

- Xing et al. (2026) report the discovery of new theropod fossil material from the Lower Jurassic [Fengjiahe Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fengjiahe_Formation) (Yunnan, China), including a tooth of a theropod distinct from *[Sinosaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinosaurus)* and two morphotypes of theropod tracks.

  [^cite_note-129]

- Lallensack et al. (2026) reevaluate factors influencing shapes of theropod tracks from the [Middle Jurassic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Jurassic) of [El Mers Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Mers_Group) ([Morocco](19291.md)) and from the [Lower Cretaceous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_Cretaceous) [Cameros Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameros_Basin?action=edit\&redlink=1) ([Spain](26667.md)), and interpret the type ichnospecies of the ichnogenera *[Saurexallopus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saurexallopus)*, *[Magnoavipes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magnoavipes)*, *[Theroplantigrada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theroplantigrada?action=edit\&redlink=1)*, *[Ordexallopus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordexallopus?action=edit\&redlink=1)* and *[Archaeornithipus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeornithipus)* as *[nomina dubia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nomen_dubium)*.

  [^cite_note-130]

- Dridi (2026) reports the discovery of new theropod tracks from the Callovian strata of the El Miit Member of the [Foum Tataouine Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foum_Tataouine_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) (Tunisia), providing evidence of more persistent presence of theropods in the studied area throughout the Jurassic than indicated by earlier discoveries.

  [^cite_note-131]

- Gesualdi et al. (2026) assign a theropod trackway from the Tithonian strata from the Plagne tracksite (France) to the ichnospecies *[Megalosauripus transjuranicus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megalosauripus)*, extending the known geographical range of this ichnotaxon.

  [^cite_note-132]

- Grallatorid tracks representing two morphotypes, associated with microbial mats that might have contributed to the formation and preservation of the tracks, are identified in the strata of the [Tuchengzi Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuchengzi_Formation) from the Jurassic-Cretaceous transition (Hebei, China) by Xing et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-133]

- Evidence from the study of theropod footprints from the Lower Cretaceous [Enciso Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enciso_Group) (Spain), indicating that differences in morphology of the studied footprints reflect distinct phases of running involving different foot postures and load distributions, is presented by Díaz-Martínez et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-134]

- Theropod tracks representing two distinct morphotypes, including tracks providing possible evidence of gregarious behavior of ornithomimosaurs, are described from the Cretaceous [Chaochuan Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaochuan_Formation) (Zhejiang, China) by Zheng et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-135]

- Charles, Polet & Hutchinson (2026) reconstruct the optimal jumping performance of *[Coelophysis bauri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coelophysis)* as overall similar to that of extant [elegant crested tinamou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elegant_crested_tinamou), but achieved through different joint kinematics and muscle work throughout the hindlimbs, and report evidence of potential impact of the long, mobile tail on jumping performance.

  [^cite_note-136]

- Redescription of the skeletal anatomy and a study on the affinities of *[Panguraptor lufengensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panguraptor)* is published by Zhang et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-137]

- A new non-[averostran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Averostra) theropod specimen distinct from *[Sinosaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinosaurus)*, preserved with evidence of presence of [tetanuran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tetanurae)-like skeletal characters, is described from the Lower Jurassic [Lufeng Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lufeng_Formation) (Yunnan, China) by Wang et al. (2026), who also name the new clade [Averostriformes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Averostriformes) that includes all neotheropods more closely related to *Allosaurus fragilis* than to *Coelophysis bauri*.

  [^cite_note-138]

- Oswald & Curtice (2026) report evidence of similarities between the morphometrics of teeth of *[Ceratosaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceratosaurus)* and upper canines of [machairodontines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machairodontinae), and argue that the dentition of the former might have been an adaptation to quick-killing middle-sized prey.

  [^cite_note-139]

- Rowe, Cerroni & [Rayfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emily_Rayfield) (2026) study mechanical performance of skulls of *Ceratosaurus*, *[Masiakasaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masiakasaurus)*, *[Carnotaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnotaurus)* and *[Majungasaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majungasaurus)*, and report evidence of adaptations of skulls of large abelisaurs to resist feeding-induced loads, suggestive of similarity of ecological roles of abelisaurs and tyrannosaurs, as well as possible evidence of adaptation of *Masiakasaurus* to capture of small prey.

  [^cite_note-140]

- Seculi Pereyra et al. (2026) review the history of studies on abelisaurid phylogeny and provide recommendations for future studies.

  [^cite_note-141]

- Seculi Pereyra (2026) studies the evolution of abelisaurid orbit shape, interpreted as more likely influenced by selective pressures such as those related to specialized predation than by phylogenetic constraints.

  [^cite_note-142]

- Seculi Pereyra, Soto & Perez (2026) study the evolution of skull ornamentation in abelisaurids, interpreted as following directional patterns and possibly driven by sexual selection.

  [^cite_note-143]

- Probable teeth of majungasaurine abelisaurids from the Cenomanian strata of the Algora site (Province of Guadalajara, Spain) are identified by López-Miguel et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-144]

- Boschetto et al. (2026) describe an abelisaurid tooth from the Upper Cretaceous (Campanian-Maastrichtian) [Argiles et Grès à Reptiles Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argiles_et_Gr%C3%A8s_%C3%A0_Reptiles_Formation) (France), preserved with a bite mark interpreted as most likely self-inflicted during carcass consumption.

  [^cite_note-145]

- Description of the anatomy of the skull of *[Skorpiovenator bustingorryi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skorpiovenator)* is published by Canale et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-146]

- Valdéz et al. (2026) report evidence of polycamerate internal structure and an advanced degree of pneumaticity in the fifth cervical vertebra of *[Viavenator exxoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viavenator)*.

  [^cite_note-147]

- Pradelli et al. (2026) describe the anatomy of the axial skeleton of *[Piatnitzkysaurus floresi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piatnitzkysaurus_floresi)*.

  [^cite_note-148]

- Li et al. (2026) report the discovery of an isolated theropod tooth from the Lower Cretaceous [Lianmuqin Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lianmuqin_Formation) (China), interpreted as evidence indicating that, in addition to *[Kelmayisaurus petrolicus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kelmayisaurus)* and small coelurosaurs, the theropod assemblage from the studied formation also included a medium-sized [tetanuran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tetanurae).

  [^cite_note-149]

- A study on the skull anatomy of spinosaurids, providing evidence of the presence of osteological correlates of supraorbitally placed salt glands similar to but evolved independently from salt glands seen in birds, is published by [Cau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_Cau) et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-150]

- Evidence from the study of the neurovascular system in spinosaurid premaxillae, interpreted as consistent with sensitivity of spinosaurid snouts and high rate of tooth replacement in members of the group, is presented by Pittet (2026).

  [^cite_note-151]

- [Buffetaut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89ric_Buffetaut) et al. (2026) report evidence of the presence of two morphotypes of spinosaurid teeth in the Lower Cretaceous strata from the Phra Prong locality (Thailand), including a morphotype with no enamel ribbing, and interpret the studied teeth as indicative of the coexistence of at least two spinosaurid taxa at this locality.

  [^cite_note-152]

- An isolated theropod tooth with possible [metriacanthosaurid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metriacanthosauridae) affinities is reported from the Upper Jurassic–Lower Cretaceous strata of the [Phu Kradung Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phu_Kradung_Formation) ([Thailand](30128.md)) by Samathi, Suteethorn & Suteethorn (2026).

  [^cite_note-153]

- Malafaia, Maggia & Rauhut (2026) describe a maxilla of a hatchling *[Allosaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allosaurus)* from the Upper Jurassic strata from the Guimarota coal mine (Portugal) with a corrosion pattern interpreted as caused by digestion, most likely resulting from consumption by a non-avian theropod.

  [^cite_note-154]

- Two partial braincases of *[Siamraptor suwati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siamraptor)*, providing new information on the cranial anatomy of the species, are described from the Lower Cretaceous [Khok Kruat Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khok_Kruat_Formation) (Thailand) by Hattori et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-155]

- Seculi Pereyra & Ibiricu (2026) report evidence of coupled evolution of shape and size of manual claws of megaraptorids, and interpret the studied theropods as likely utilizing forelimbs in a specialized predation strategy.

  [^cite_note-156]

- Aranciaga-Rolando (2026) links the increase of skeletal pneumaticity throughout the evolutionary history of megaraptorids to reduction of body mass in members of this group compared to theropods of comparable body size from other groups.

  [^cite_note-157]

- Nielsen et al. (2026) identify tooth marks on a tyrannosaurid metatarsal BDM 124 from the Judith River Formation (Montana, United States) as produced by a small-bodied, likely juvenile tyrannosaurid scavenging on a larger individual.

  [^cite_note-158]

- McElvogue & Lehman (2026) describe tyrannosaur feeding traces on hadrosaur limb bones from the [Aguja Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aguja_Formation) (Texas, United States), interpreting the limbs as removed from the carcass to a separate feeding site, and interpreting the traces as indicative of a substantial effort to remove all flesh from ankles and feet.

  [^cite_note-159]

- Evidence of preservation of micro- and nanoscale histological features (including Haversian canal and lacunocanalicular network permineralization) in bones of *[Albertosaurus sarcophagus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albertosaurus)* from the [Horseshoe Canyon Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horseshoe_Canyon_Formation) (Alberta, Canada) is presented by Williams et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-160]

- A natural mold of a partial rostrum of a juvenile specimen of *[Gorgosaurus libratus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gorgosaurus)* is described from the Campanian strata of the [Wapiti Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wapiti_Formation) (British Columbia, Canada) by Coppock, Larsson & [Currie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philip_J._Currie) (2026), extending the known geographical range of the species.

  [^cite_note-161]

- Longrich et al. (2026) report the discovery of a tibia of a large-bodied tyrannosaurid from the Campanian strata of the [Kirtland Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirtland_Formation) (New Mexico, United States), interpreted as likely to be a bone of an early representative of [Tyrannosaurini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyrannosaurini).

  [^cite_note-162]

- Yun (2026) studies the affinities of the tyrannosaurid maxilla TMM 41436-1 from the [Javelina Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javelina_Formation) (Texas, United States), and interprets the available evidence as insufficient for placement of the studied specimen in a tyrannosaurid taxon distinct from *Tyrannosaurus rex*.

  [^cite_note-163]

- Raun et al. (2026) revise proposed diagnostic characters of *Asiatyrannus xui* and *Raptorex kriegsteini*, and interpret the type material of both taxa as likely to be juvenile specimens of *[Tarbosaurus bataar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarbosaurus)*.

  [^cite_note-164]

- [Woodward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holly_Woodward_Ballard), [Myhrvold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nathan_Myhrvold) & [Horner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Horner_\(paleontologist\)) (2026) reconstruct the life history of *[Tyrannosaurus](30467.md)* on the basis of bone histology, reporting evidence of a more gradual annual growth rate slope than indicated by earlier studies and evidence of a protracted subadult stage, and find that growth trajectories of the tyrannosaur specimens BMRP 2002.4.1 (the holotype of *[Nanotyrannus lethaeus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanotyrannus)*) and BMRP 2006.4.4 did not fit the *T. rex* growth curve model.

  [^cite_note-165]

- A study on the locomotion of *Tyrannosaurus*, indicative of similarity of foot-strike patterns to those of the ostrich, is published by Boeye et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-166]

- A trackway including four large theropod tracks, likely produced by an adult *Tyrannosaurus rex*, is described from the Maastrichtian strata of the Hell Creek Formation (North Dakota, United States) by Falkingham et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-167]

- Flores et al. (2026) determine body temperatures of *Tyrannosaurus rex* from the Hell Creek Formation (Montana, United States) and contemporary crocodilians on the basis of the study of clumped isotopes from their tooth enamel, and report evidence that the body temperature of *T. rex* was higher than its environment and comparable to those of extant [endotherms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endotherm).

  [^cite_note-168]

- Longrich et al. (2026) identify fossil material of hatchlings and young juveniles of *Gorgosaurus libratus* and *Tyrannosaurus rex* from the Upper Cretaceous formations in the United States and Canada, interpreted as indicative of small body size of tyrannosaurid hatchlings and their precociality; the authors also interpret tooth wear of hatchlings as indicating that they fed on relatively large vertebrates, and estimate that tyrannosaurids were capable of producing large clutches of eggs.

  [^cite_note-169]

- New theropod vertebrae, including fossil material of a troodontid and a probable ornithomimosaurian, are described from the Upper Cretaceous [Iren Dabasu Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iren_Dabasu_Formation) (China) by Xu et al. (2026), expanding known morphological diversity of theropod vertebrae from this formation.

  [^cite_note-170]

- [Arbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Arbour), Bullard & [Evans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_C._Evans_\(paleontologist\)) (2026) report the discovery of an ornithomimosaur caudal vertebra from the Campanian [Cedar District Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cedar_District_Formation), representing the first confirmed dinosaur record from outcrops of the [Nanaimo Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanaimo_Group) in Canada and the second confirmed record of dinosaur fossil material from the Nanaimo Group overall.

  [^cite_note-171]

- Calvo-Pérez & Cuesta (2026) study the neuroanatomy of the endocranial cavity of *[Pelecanimimus polyodon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pelecanimimus)*, and report evidence of similarity of endocranial morphology to those of other ornithomimosaurs.

  [^cite_note-172]

- Libke et al. (2026) support the interpretation of isolated dentaries from the Upper Cretaceous [Judith River Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judith_River_Formation) ([Montana](19978.md), United States) described by Chinzorig et al. (2025)

  [^cite_note-173]

  &#x20;as belonging to an aberrantly large ornithomimosaur, but argue that the studied fossils cannot be confidently assigned to a member of the family [Deinocheiridae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deinocheiridae).

  [^cite_note-174]

- Makovicky et al. (2026) report the discovery of a new specimen of *[Alnashetri cerropoliciensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alnashetri)* from the [Candeleros Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candeleros_Formation) (Argentina), providing new information on the anatomy of members of this species; the authors also interpret the Late Jurassic theropod vertebra described by Makovicky (1997),

  [^cite_note-175]

  &#x20;the theropod astragalus YPM 9163 from [Como Bluff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Como_Bluff) ([Morrison Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morrison_Formation), Wyoming, United States; formerly referred to *[Coelurus fragilis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coelurus)*) and *[Calamosaurus foxi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calamosaurus)* as [alvarezsauroids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alvarezsauroidea), and study the phylogenetic relationships and evolutionary history of members of this group, interpreting it as having [Pangaean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pangaea) ancestral distribution.

  [^cite_note-176]

- A study on the functional capacity of forelimbs of alvarezsauroids, supporting their adaptations for digging, is published by Leedham et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-177]

- Meso et al. (2026) redescribe the anatomy of the [holotype](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holotype) specimen of *[Bonapartenykus ultimus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bonapartenykus)*.

  [^cite_note-178]

- Wang et al. (2026) report the first discovery of a [maniraptoran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maniraptora) egg from the Cenomanian [Quantou Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quantou_Formation) (Jilin, China), and name a new ootaxon *[Jilinoolithus lamellotestus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jilinoolithus?action=edit\&redlink=1)*.

  [^cite_note-179]

- The first clutch of non-avian dinosaur eggs from the Cretaceous Dengta Basin (Guangdong, China), assigned to the ootaxon *[Elongatoolithus elongatus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elongatoolithus)*, is described by Wang et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-180]

- A study on the biomechanics of the forelimbs of *[Falcarius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falcarius)* is published by Smith (2026).

  [^cite_note-181]

- Freimuth, Lively & Zanno (2026) reinterpret *"Geminiraptor" suarezarum* as a member of Therizinosauria and as a species belonging to the genus *Falcarius*.

  [^cite_note-182]

- A nearly complete [pes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pes_\(anatomy\)) of a [caudipterid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caudipterid) oviraptorosaur that did not reach complete somatic maturity, providing new information on the anatomy of members of this group, is described from the Lower Cretaceous Yixian Formation (China) by Han et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-183]

- Description of the anatomy of the postcranial skeleton of *[Gigantoraptor erlianensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigantoraptor)* is published by Hao, Tan & [Xu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xu_Xing_\(paleontologist\)) (2026).

  [^cite_note-184]

- Description of the inner ear anatomy of *[Epichirostenotes curriei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epichirostenotes)* and two [caenagnathid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caenagnathidae) specimens from the Judith River and Hell Creek formations (Montana, United States) is published by Bazzana-Adams, Funston & [Evans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_C._Evans_\(paleontologist\)) (2026).

  [^cite_note-185]

- Su et al. (2026) determine heat transfer during the incubation of a clutch of oviraptorid eggs on the basis of incubation experiments, and find that oviraptorid parents transferred heat to their eggs less efficiently than extant birds and depended in part on environmental heat sources for incubation.

  [^cite_note-186]

- Hao & [Xu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xu_Xing_\(paleontologist\)) (2026) report the discovery of a new nest-associated oviraptorid specimen from the Upper Cretaceous strata from Jiangxi (China), and interpret *[Huanansaurus ganzhouensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huanansaurus)*, *Corythoraptor jacobsi* and the new specimen as likely representing a single species.

  [^cite_note-187]

- Fossil material of probable non-avialan members of [Paraves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paraves), including parts of ulna and humerus that might belong to a single, approximately turkey-sized individual, is described from the Barremian [Papo Seco Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papo_Seco_Formation) (Portugal) by Figueiredo et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-188]

- Hefler et al. (2026) study the aerodynamics of *[Microraptor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microraptor)* during flight, reporting evidence of beneficial impact of forewing–hindwing interactions on flow dynamics.

  [^cite_note-189]

- The first deinonychosaurian (probably troodontid) track from [Japan](15573.md) is described from the Lower Cretaceous [Kitadani Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kitadani_Formation) by Tsukiji, Hattori & Azuma (2026).

  [^cite_note-190]

- A probable troodontid tooth with similarities to *[Troodon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troodon)* is described from the Upper Cretaceous strata of the [Kallamedu Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kallamedu_Formation) (India) by Dhiman et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-191]

- Yin et al. (2026) study the external and internal anatomy of two troodontid teeth from the Maastrichtian [Yuliangzi Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuliangzi_Formation) (Heilongjiang, China), including a new specimen with similarities to *Troodon*.

  [^cite_note-192]

- A new postcranial skeleton of *[Sinovenator changii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinovenator)*, providing new information on the anatomy of members of this species and extending their known geographical distribution, is described from the Lower Cretaceous strata of the Ningcheng Basin (Inner Mongolia, China) by Yu et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-193]

- Description of the pelvic and hindlimb anatomy of embryonic to skeletally mature troodontid specimens (possibly representing the species *Troodon formosus*) from the Upper Cretaceous Two Medicine Formation (Montana, United States) is published by Varricchio & Caldwell (2026).

  [^cite_note-194]

- Serio et al. (2026) present a reconstruction of the forelimb of *Troodon formosus*, and reconstruct its range of motion.

  [^cite_note-195]

- Review of evidence of troodontid dietary habits is published by Fan, Miller & Pittman (2026).

  [^cite_note-196]

- Li et al. (2026) redescribe the [holotype](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holotype) of *Eosinopteryx brevipenna* and interpret this species as a [junior synonym](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synonym_\(taxonomy\)) of *[Anchiornis huxleyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anchiornis)*.

  [^cite_note-197]

- García-Gil et al. (2026) identify isolated theropod teeth from the Upper Cretaceous [El Gallo Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Gallo_Formation) ([Mexico](3966054.md)) as belonging to dromaeosaurids, troodontids, maniraptorans of uncertain affinities and indeterminate theropods.

  [^cite_note-198]

#### Sauropodomorph research

- Schuurman, Bruner & Knoll (2026) reconstruct the topology of skulls of 14 sauropodomorph species spanning the entire evolutionary history of the group, and report probable evidence of limitation of morphological changes of individual bones by spatial constraints.

  [^cite_note-199]

- A study on the evolution of the humeral morphology of sauropodomorphs, providing evidence of different pattern of evolution of sauropod-like traits in the proximal and distal half of the humerus, is published by Lefebvre et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-200]

- Evidence from the study of the fossil record of sauropodomorph tracks, indicative of changes through time related to increase of body size in the morphology of the sauropodomorph [manus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manus_\(anatomy\)) (but not in the morphology of the [pes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pes_\(anatomy\))), is presented by Yamaguchi, Kubo & Kubo (2026).

  [^cite_note-201]

- A footprint and an associated tail trace that were probably produced by a bipedal sauropodomorph, representing the oldest dinosaur trace fossil from Australia reported to date, are described from the Carnian strata of the [Aspley Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aspley_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) in Queensland by Romilio & Runnegar (2026).

  [^cite_note-202]

- D'Orazi Porchetti et al. (2026) assign tetradactyl footprints from the Carnian strata of the [Hassberge Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hassberge_Formation) (Germany) to the ichnogenus *[Evazoum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evazoum?action=edit\&redlink=1)*, and interpret the studied footprints as likely produced by bipedal sauropodomorphs.

  [^cite_note-203]

- Campos et al. (2026) describe the fossil material and study the bone histology of a small-bodied, juvenile [sauropodomorph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sauropodomorpha) from the Upper Triassic strata from the Cerro da Alemoa site ([Santa Maria Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Maria_Formation), [Brazil](3383.md)), representing the smallest well-preserved skeletal remains of a sauropodomorph from Brazil reported to date.

  [^cite_note-204]

- Description of the anatomy of the axial skeleton and a study on the affinities of *[Macrocollum itaquii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macrocollum)* is published by Fonseca et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-205]

- Xing et al. (2026) report the discovery of probable sauropodomorph tracks from a new tracksite from the [Upper Triassic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Triassic) [Xujiahe Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xujiahe_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) ([Sichuan](65185.md), [China](5405.md)).

  [^cite_note-206]

- Chen et al. (2026) determine the oldest sauropodomorph fossils from the [Kunming Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kunming_Basin?action=edit\&redlink=1) (Yunnan, China) to be 200.17-million-years-old, and interpret this result as evidence of colonization of low palaeolatitude area of southwest China by medium- to large-bodied dinosaurs in the aftermath of the [Triassic–Jurassic extinction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triassic%E2%80%93Jurassic_extinction).

  [^cite_note-207]

- Evidence from the study of tooth morphology and replacement patterns, indicative of diverse feeding ecologies of Early Jurassic sauropods from the Cañadón Asfalto Basin (Argentina), is presented by Gomez, Carballido & Pol (2026).

  [^cite_note-208]

- Ren et al. (2026) report the discovery of dorsal vertebrae of a large-bodied sauropod (possibly a [basal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basal_\(phylogenetics\)) [neosauropod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neosauropoda)) from the Middle Jurassic (Aalenian) strata of the [Xintiangou Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xintiangou_Formation) (China).

  [^cite_note-209]

- Saleiro et al. (2026) present the workflow behind the creation of a large dataset of surface texture measurements of teeth of Late Jurassic sauropods.

  [^cite_note-210]

- Chowchuvech et al. (2026) study the affinities of isolated sauropod teeth from the [Khorat Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khorat_Group) (Thailand), interpreted as indicative of a shift in composition of local sauropod assemblages across the Jurassic–Cretaceous.

  [^cite_note-211]

- The largest sauropod tracksite from the Lower Cretaceous [Madongshan Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madongshan_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) (Ningxia, China), preserving tracks with a morphology intermediate between those typical of *Brontopodus* and *Parabrontopodus* tracks, is described by Yang et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-212]

- Sauropod tracks produced in wet aeolian environmental, possibly while the trackmakers travelled towards a habitat with greater resource availability, are described from the Lower Cretaceous [Três Barras Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tr%C3%AAs_Barras_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) (Brazil) by Nascimento et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-213]

- Kubo et al. (2026) study tooth wear of a specimen of *[Omeisaurus maoianus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omeisaurus)*, finding no evidence of significant differences of microwear texture in the same individual based on tooth position, and report evidence of tooth wear differences between *[Yunnanosaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yunnanosaurus)* and derived sauropods that were likely related to dietary differences.

  [^cite_note-214]

- Shui, Shao & Yin (2026) present the first three-dimensional reconstruction of a mamenchisaurid tooth based on data from a new specimen from the Upper Jurassic [Qigu Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qigu_Formation) (China), interpreted as likely representing a taxon distinct from the specimen reported by Maisch & Matzke (2019).

  [^cite_note-215]

  [^cite_note-216]

- A study on the bone histology of cervical ribs of a juvenile mamenchisaurid specimen from the Jurassic [Shaximiao Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaximiao_Formation) (Sichuan, China) is published by Zhou et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-217]

- Ghosh et al. (2026) describe a probable turiasaur tooth from the Bathonian strata of the Jaisalmer Basin ([India](14533.md)), interpreted as the oldest record of group from the Indian Subcontinent reported to date.

  [^cite_note-218]

- Casts of sauropod teeth from a private quarry near Skull Creek in northwestern Colorado (United States) interpreted as the first record of a member of [Turiasauria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turiasauria) from the Upper Jurassic [Morrison Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morrison_Formation) are described by [Foster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_R._Foster), Woodruff & Royo-Torres (2026).

  [^cite_note-219]

- Gouellec et al. (2026) study dental morphotypes in the turiasaur from the [Angeac-Charente bonebed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angeac-Charente_bonebed) (France), reporting evidence of [heterodonty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heterodont).

  [^cite_note-220]

- A study on the caudal vertebrae of *[Cetiosaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cetiosaurus)* sp., *"Cetiosaurus" glymptonensis*, *[Wamweracaudia keranjei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wamweracaudia)*, *[Dicraeosaurus sattleri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dicraeosaurus)*, *[Tornieria africana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tornieria)* and *[Giraffatitan brancai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giraffatitan)*, providing evidence of at least five independent origins and/or reversals of pneumaticity of anterior caudal vertebrae in Neosauropoda, is published by Beeston et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-221]

- Foster et al. (2026) review the history of excavation and study of the fossil material of *[Dystrophaeus viaemalae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dystrophaeus)*, review the geological setting of the fossil material, and interpret the morphology of the fossil material of *D. viaemalae* (including additional material collected since 2014) as unlikely to be a member of Diplodocoidea.

  [^cite_note-222]

- Sánchez-Fenollosa, García-Cobeña & Cobos (2026) report the discovery of fossil material of a member of the genus *[Diplodocus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diplodocus)* from the Upper Jurassic strata of the [Villar del Arzobispo Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villar_del_Arzobispo_Formation) (Spain), extending known geographical range of the genus beyond North America.

  [^cite_note-223]

- The study by Gallagher et al. (2025) reporting evidence of preservation of microbodies within epidermis in the scales of juvenile specimens of *Diplodocus* sp. from the [Mother's Day Quarry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother's_Day_Quarry), ([Morrison Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morrison_Formation); Montana, United States), which they identify as probable fossil melanosomes,

  [^cite_note-224]

  &#x20;is criticized by Rossi & McNamara (2026).

  [^cite_note-225]

- Lerzo (2026) reevaluates *[Nopcsaspondylus alarconensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nopcsaspondylus)* and considers it to be a *[nomen dubium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nomen_dubium)*.

  [^cite_note-226]

- The sauropod specimen MMCh-PV 47 from the [Candeleros Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candeleros_Formation) (Argentina), originally described as a titanosaur by Otero et al. (2011),

  [^cite_note-227]

  &#x20;is interpreted as a [rebbachisaurid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebbachisauridae) by Lerzo (2026), providing new information on the tail musculature of members of this group.

  [^cite_note-228]

- Garderes, Lerzo & Knoll (2026) study the endocranial morphology of *[Sidersaura marae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sidersaura)*, and report evidence indicating that rebbachisaurids might have differed from other sauropods in the variation in hearing capabilities relative to body size.

  [^cite_note-229]

- Rego et al. (2026) study the taphonomy of remains of *[Itapeuasaurus cajapioensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Itapeuasaurus)* preserved in transitional to marginal-marine depositional context of the [Alcântara Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alc%C3%A2ntara_Formation) (Brazil).

  [^cite_note-230]

- A study on changes of diversity of [Diplodocoidea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diplodocoidea) and its subgroups during the Jurassic-Cretaceous transition is published by Órfão et al. (2026), who reports evidence of two distinct peaks of diversity in Late Jurassic and Early Cretaceous, and evidence of distinct patterns of geographic distributions of [Flagellicaudata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flagellicaudata) and Rebbachisauridae.

  [^cite_note-231]

- Garderes et al. (2026) present a reconstruction of the cranial musculature of *[Bajadasaurus pronuspinax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bajadasaurus)*.

  [^cite_note-232]

- Hullinger et al. (2026) describe [apatosaurine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apatosaurinae) remains from [Arches National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arches_National_Park) ([Utah](31716.md), United States), representing a medium-sized yet geologically young member of the group.

  [^cite_note-hullinger2026-233]

- A vertebra interpreted as the northernmost record of *[Barosaurus lentus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barosaurus)* from the Morrison Formation reported to date is described from the Pryor Mountains (Montana, United States) by Woodruff et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-234]

- van der Linden et al. (2026) report on a specimen of *Barosaurus* with a pathology in the "whip" part of the tail.

  [^cite_note-235]

- [Carpenter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenneth_Carpenter), Ikejiri & Wilson (2026) study the anatomy of postcranial skeleton of two immature specimens of *[Camarasaurus lentus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camarasaurus)* from the strata of the Morrison Formation from [Dinosaur National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dinosaur_National_Monument) (Utah, United States), interpreted as representing sequential stages in the ontogeny of the species and providing new information on its morphological variability.

  [^cite_note-236]

- Yoshida, [Carpenter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenneth_Carpenter) & Kobayashi (2026) report the discovery of fossil material of members of [Somphospondyli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somphospondyli) from the [Cedar Mountain Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cedar_Mountain_Formation) (Utah, United States), providing evidence of coexistence of members of this group and brachiosaurids in North America during the [Aptian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aptian).

  [^cite_note-237]

- A new dinosaur tracksite dominated by tracks of sauropods (probably non-titanosaurian members of Somphospondyli) is described from the Lower Cretaceous strata of the [Wawukuang Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wawukuang_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) (Shandong, China) by Ren et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-238]

- Averianov et al. (2026) describe a partial fibula of a probable member of [Euhelopodidae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euhelopodidae) from the Lower Cretaceous [Ilek Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilek_Formation) (Kemerovo Oblast, Russia), interpreted as evidence of presence of two sauropod taxa at the Shestakovo 3 locality.

  [^cite_note-239]

- A study on the bone histology and growth patterns of *[Malarguesaurus florenciae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malarguesaurus)* is published by Previtera (2026).

  [^cite_note-240]

- Redescription of the anatomy and a study on the affinities of *[Triunfosaurus leonardii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triunfosaurus)* is published by Mannion & Carvalho (2026), who interpret the studied species as a member of Somphospondyli belonging or closely related to Titanosauria.

  [^cite_note-241]

- Iangrai et al. (2026) report the discovery of probable titanosaur remains from the Upper Cretaceous [Mahadek Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahadek_Formation) (India).

  [^cite_note-242]

- New titanosaur fossil material, including one of the largest femora of *[Jainosaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jainosaurus)* reported to date, is described from the Maastrichtian [Lameta Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lameta_Formation) (India) by Kumar et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-243]

- Pal & Deshmukh (2026) described an ulna and radius of two titanosaur specimens discovered from [Lameta Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lameta_Formation) ([India](14533.md)), with the first osteohistological description of a juvenile specimen from the country.

  [^cite_note-244]

- Averianov et al. (2026) describe the first cervical vertebra referable to *[Tengrisaurus starkovi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tengrisaurus_starkovi)*, and recover it as a [basal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basal_\(phylogenetics\)) member of [Colossosauria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colossosauria) in an updated phylogenetic study including this new material.

  [^cite_note-245]

- Fossil material of a non-saltasaurine titanosaur (possibly a member of Colossosauria), representing the first dinosaur record from the Oriente Basin at the Upper Cretaceous (Maastrichtian) [Tena Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tena_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) (Ecuador), is described by Balcázar-Loaiza et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-246]

- Pérez Moreno et al. (2026) revise the fossil material attributed to *[Muyelensaurus pecheni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muyelensaurus)*, interpret it as belonging to sauropods from more than one taxon, and restrict *M. pecheni* to the [holotype](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holotype) specimen only.

  [^cite_note-247]

- Navarro et al. (2026) describe a titanosaur [axis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Axis_\(anatomy\)) with possible [lognkosaurian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lognkosauria) affinities from the Upper Cretaceous [São José do Rio Preto Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Jos%C3%A9_do_Rio_Preto_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) (Brazil), providing evidence of presence of a sauropod with body dimensions comparable to those of *[Futalognkosaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Futalognkosaurus)* in the [Bauru Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bauru_Group) prior to the Campanian, and report evidence of presence of phylogenetically informative character in the sauropod axis vertebrae.

  [^cite_note-248]

- A caudal vertebra of a small-bodied titanosaur (probably a non-saltasaurid member of [Eutitanosauria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eutitanosauria)) is described from the Upper Cretaceous (Campanian) strata of the [Santa Marta Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Marta_Formation) (James Ross Island, Antarctica) by Barrett et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-249]

- A study on changes in the skeleton of *[Neuquensaurus australis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuquensaurus)*, based on data from the skeleton of a juvenile individual, is published by Zurriaguz et al. (2026), who also study the bone histology of the new individual, reporting evidence of an overall rapid growth, but also evidence of at least one cyclical growth mark in the cortex.

  [^cite_note-250]

- Capurro & Fernández (2026) study the structure of shells of titanosaur eggs from the [Auca Mahuevo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auca_Mahuevo) site (Argentina), reporting evidence that the studied eggshells had comparable values of water vapour conductance and porosity to eggshells of extant archosaurs incubating their eggs in open nests.

  [^cite_note-251]

- A bowl-shaped depression with scratch and claw traces, interpreted as likely resulting from repeated sauropod foot movement and possibly representing an abandoned nest dug by a titanosaur sauropod, is reported from the Maastrichtian strata of the [El Molino Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Molino_Formation) (Bolivia) by Meyer et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-252]

- Tanaka et al. (2026) report the discovery of shells of titanosaur eggs from the Maastrichtian strata of the [Chorrillo Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chorrillo_Formation) in southernmost Patagonia (Argentina), providing evidence of sauropod nesting at highest palaeolatitude in the Southern Hemisphere reported to date, and interpret the studied eggs as buried in a mound-style nest and incubated with heat from decaying vegetation.

  [^cite_note-253]

- Alessandretti et al. (2026) describe sauropod undertracks from the Upper Cretaceous [Capacete Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capacete_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) (Brazil), determine the environmental conditions that resulted in their formation and preservation, and interpret the sedimentological and paleontological data from the Sanfranciscana Basin coupled with reconstructions of Late Cretaceous climate as suggestive of sauropod migrations from the Bauru Basin to the Sanfranciscana Basin.

  [^cite_note-254]

- Bellardini et al. (2026) describe new sauropod fossil material from the [Allen Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allen_Formation) (Argentina), providing evidence of presence of sauropods in southeastern Neuquén Basin at the time of environmental of changes in northern Patagonia linked with the ocean transgression during the Maastrichtian, and providing possible evidence of presence of a sauropod taxon distinct from *Aeolosaurus rionegrinus*, *Bonatitan*, *Menucocelsior* and *Rocasaurus* in the Allen Formation.

  [^cite_note-255]

### Ornithischian research

- Reid, Fonseca & Madzia (2026) evaluate the history of ornithischian clade names and their synonymy.

  [^cite_note-256]

- A study on the life history of *[Changchunsaurus parvus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Changchunsaurus)*, providing evidence of one of the slowest growth rates reported in dinosaurs, is published by Wang et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-257]

- Avrahami & [Zanno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lindsay_Zanno) (2026) describe the anatomy of the cranial elements of *[Fona herzogae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fona)*.

  [^cite_note-258]

- Ornithischian fossil material including caudal vertebrae with similarities to the vertebrae of *[Psittacosaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psittacosaurus)*, representing the westernmost record of a dinosaur from the Lower Cretaceous [Bayin-Gobi Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayin-Gobi_Formation) (China) reported to date, is described by Li et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-259]

#### Thyreophoran research

- Sánchez-Fenollosa, Cobos & Suñer (2026) describe new stegosaurian fossil material from the [Villar del Arzobispo Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villar_del_Arzobispo_Formation) (Spain), and revise the fossil record of Stegosauria from the eastern part of the Iberian Peninsula.

  [^cite_note-260]

- [Hunt-Foster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ReBecca_Hunt-Foster) et al. (2026) describe portions of forelimbs of an indeterminate stegosaurid from the Brushy Basin Member of the Morrison Formation (Utah, United States), estimated to be the largest stegosaurid specimen from the Morrison Formation reported to date.

  [^cite_note-261]

- Costa (2026) identifies five additional occurrences of dacentrurine stegosaur fossils (besides the holotype of *[Alcovasauru&#x73;*/*&#x4D;iragaia longispinus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcovasaurus)*) in the Upper Jurassic strata of the Morrison Formation (United States).

  [^cite_note-262]

- A juvenile specimen representing the smallest individual of *[Stegosaurus stenops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stegosaurus)* reported to date is described from the strata of the Morrison Formation in Wyoming (United States) by Carpenter (2026).

  [^cite_note-263]

- New thyreophoran fossil material with probable stegosaurian affinities is described from the Lower Cretaceous (Berriasian–Valanginian) [Bajada Colorada Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bajada_Colorada_Formation) (Argentina) by Riguetti et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-264]

- Agnolín et al. (2026) report the discovery of new fossil material of *[Patagopelta cristata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patagopelta)*, providing new information on the anatomy of members of this species and supporting its placement within Parankylosauria.

  [^cite_note-265]

- Cross et al. (2026) modify and develop new tooth characters for ankylosaur systematics, and study the phylogenetic relationships of members of this group.

  [^cite_note-266]

- New information on the internal anatomy of the skull of *[Panoplosaurus mirus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panoplosaurus)* is provided by Livius et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-267]

- Yoon et al. (2026) identify probable ankylosaurid tracks, referred to as cf. *Ruopodosaurus*, from the Cenomanian [Jindong Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jindong_Formation) ([South Korea](27019.md)).

  [^cite_note-268]

- Zhu et al. (2026) describe new fossil material of *[Pinacosaurus grangeri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinacosaurus)* from the Upper Cretaceous [Wulansuhai Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wulansuhai_Formation) (China), refine the diagnosis of this species, study changes in its skull morphology during its life history, and report possible evidence of sexual dimorphism.

  [^cite_note-269]

#### Cerapod research

- Kim, Lee & Gihm (2026) report the discovery of a trackway of a running ornithopod from the Lower Cretaceous (Aptian–Albian) strata of the [Hasandong Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasandong_Formation) (South Korea).

  [^cite_note-270]

- Rotatori et al. (2026) study the phylogenetic relationships of [iguanodontian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iguanodontia) ornithopods and reconstruct the evolutionary history of the group, name a new clade [Ouranosauria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ouranosauria), and trace the major radiation of Iguanodontia to the Early Jurassic (Pliensbachian–Toarcian).

  [^cite_note-271]

- Naugolnykh & Afonkin (2026) report the discovery of ornithopod footprints from the Lower Cretaceous (Valanginian) strata from Stavropol Krai (Russia), and name a new ichnotaxon *[Iguanodontipus lermontovi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iguanodontipus)*.

  [^cite_note-272]

- Description of new fossil material of *[Muttaburrasaurus langdoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muttaburrasaurus)* and a study on its craniodental anatomy, palaeoneurology and sensory palaeobiology is published by Herne et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-273]

- Three [iguanodontian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iguanodontia) specimens with a morphology distinct from those of members of the genera *[Dryosaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dryosaurus)* and *[Camptosaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camptosaurus)* are described from the strata of the Morrison Formation from the Simon Quarry (Wyoming, United States) by Krumenacker et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-274]

- Rodríguez-Borges et al. (2026) describe a femur of an early juvenile iguanodontian of uncertain affinities from the Santonian [Bajo de la Carpa Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bajo_de_la_Carpa_Formation) (Argentina), and report evidence of ontogenetic and intraspecific variability of femoral characters used in the studies of the phylogenetic relationships of ornithopods.

  [^cite_note-275]

- Rocchi et al. (2026) provide new information on the anatomy of *[Dysalotosaurus lettowvorbecki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dysalotosaurus)* and changes in its skeleton during its ontogeny on the basis of the study of an immature individual from the [Tendaguru Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tendaguru_Formation) (Tanzania).

  [^cite_note-276]

- Dieudonné, Paulina-Carabajal & Cruzado-Caballero (2026) redescribe the anatomy of the skull of *[Gasparinisaura cincosaltensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gasparinisaura)*, and name a new clade [Dysalotosaurinae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dysalotosaurinae) within [Dryosauridae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dryosauridae).

  [^cite_note-277]

- [Galton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Galton) & Carpenter (2026) redescribe the anatomy of the holotype and paratypes of *Camptosaurus dispar* and the holotype of *C. medius*, and support the interpretation of *C. medius*, *C. nanus* and *C. browni* as [junior synonyms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synonym_\(taxonomy\)) of *C. dispar*.

  [^cite_note-278]

- Gônet, Allain & Houssaye (2026) determine probable locomotor preferences of *[Iguanodon bernissartensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iguanodon)*, *[Ouranosaurus nigeriensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ouranosaurus)* and *[Lurdusaurus arenatus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lurdusaurus)*, interpreting the studied taxa as likely obligate quadrupeds, and interpreting *Lurdusaurus* as the first known graviportal ornithopod.

  [^cite_note-279]

- Kondo et al. (2026) identify a sternal plate of an indeterminate member of [Iguanodontia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iguanodontia) (possibly a hadrosauroid) from the Upper Cretaceous (Turonian-Coniacian) [Ashizawa Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashizawa_Formation) (Futaba Group), belonging to an individual that was smaller than other iguanodontians reported from the Upper Cretaceous marine strata from Japan.

  [^cite_note-280]

- Salas-Herrera et al. (2026) diagnose a specimen of *Iguanodon bernissartensis* from the Barremian [Arcillas de Morella Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arcillas_de_Morella_Formation) (Spain) with a pathological overgrowth in the tibia as affected by acute overlying skin ulceration, possibly a [tropical ulcer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_ulcer).

  [^cite_note-281]

- Ma et al. (2026) report the discovery of a new skull of *[Qianjiangsaurus changshengi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qianjiangsaurus)* from the Upper Cretaceous [Zhengyang Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhengyang_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) (China), preserving evidence of presence of a hollow crest on the skull of the studied dinosaur that was structurally non-[homologous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homology_\(biology\)) with crests of lambeosaurine hadrosaurids.

  [^cite_note-282]

- Ma et al. (2026) study the taphonomy and age profile of the assemblage dominated by specimens of *[Bactrosaurus johnsoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bactrosaurus)* from the Upper Cretaceous [Iren Dabasu Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iren_Dabasu_Formation) (China) collected during fieldwork conducted in 2014 and 2015, report that the assemblage is dominated by nestling and juvenile individuals (interpreted as consistent with population segregation between juveniles and adults and with herding behavior of *B. johnsoni*), and interpret the studied fossil assemblage as likely affected by an attritional mortality pattern.

  [^cite_note-283]

- Yu et al. (2026) report the first discovery of [lambeosaurine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lambeosaurinae) [hadrosaurid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hadrosauridae) fossil material from the Campanian [Nenjiang Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nenjiang_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) (China), interpreted by the authors as supporting Asian origin of the group.

  [^cite_note-284]

- Sharpe & Brown (2026) describe fossil material of *[Hypacrosaurus altispinus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypacrosaurus)* from the [Horseshoe Canyon Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horseshoe_Canyon_Formation) (Alberta, Canada), representing the northernmost record of the species reported to date; the authors also provide evidence of morphological separation between *H. altispinus* and *"Hypacrosaurus" stebingeri*, and propose a new combination *Corythosaurus stebingeri* for the latter species.

  [^cite_note-285]

- Dudgeon, Brown & [Evans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_C._Evans_\(paleontologist\)) (2026) describe the internal crest anatomy of mature individuals of *[Corythosaurus casuarius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corythosaurus)*, *C. intermedius* and *[Lambeosaurus lambei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lambeosaurus)*.

  [^cite_note-286]

- A study on bite force and stress distribution in the skull of *Corythosaurus casuarius*, providing evidence of changes of feeding mechanics of the studied hadrosaurid during its life history, is published by Dudgeon & Evans (2026).

  [^cite_note-287]

- Hunter & [Janis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christine_Janis) (2026) compare tooth wear in juvenile and adult individuals of *[Maiasaura peeblesorum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maiasaura)*, and report evidence of differences interpreted as consistent with a shift from feeding on nutritious, low-fiber plants to feeding on nutritionally poor, high-fiber plants during the life of the studied dinosaur.

  [^cite_note-288]

- Duarte-Bigurra et al. (2026) describe partial hadrosaurid specimens from the Campanian strata of the Fronteras section of the Cabullona Group from Arroyo del Alamito and Puerto Viejo localities (Sonora, Mexico), providing evidence of presence in southern [Laramidia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laramidia) of members of the tribe [Kritosaurini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kritosaurini) related to "secernosaurs" from Patagonia (Argentina).

  [^cite_note-289]

- Saitta et al. (2026) reconstruct the taphonomic sequence of preservation of specimens of *[Edmontosaurus annectens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmontosaurus_annectens)* from the Lance Formation (Wyoming, United States) described by Sereno et al. (2025),

  [^cite_note-290]

  &#x20;and find no convincing evidence of preservation of original tissue structures or biomolecules.

  [^cite_note-291]

- Bateman & Larsson (2026) compare the cranial musculature and likely feeding performance of *[Stegoceras validum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stegoceras)* and other ornithischians, providing evidence of greater similarity of the feeding performance of *S. validum* to those of basal ornithischians and ornithopods than to that of *[Psittacosaurus lujiatunensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psittacosaurus)*, and interpret their findings as indicating that evolution of cranial domes of pachycephalosaurs constrained the evolution of their jaw musculature and their feeding performance.

  [^cite_note-292]

- Moore et al. (2026) describe postcranial remains of an indeterminate, early juvenile pachycephalosaur specimen from the Maastrichtian [Frenchman Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frenchman_Formation) (Saskatchewan, Canada), representing the ontogenetically youngest pachycephalosaur postcranium reported to date.

  [^cite_note-293]

- Maidment et al. (2026) use new remains of *[Ajkaceratops kozmai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajkaceratops)* from the Late Cretaceous [Csehbánya Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Csehb%C3%A1nya_Formation) ([Hungary](13275.md)) to conclude that this species is confidently a [ceratopsian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceratopsia), "*Mochlodon*" *vorosi* is a [junior synonym](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_synonym) of this species, and [Late Cretaceous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Cretaceous) Europe preserves a previously unrecognized diversity of horned dinosaurs represented by taxa otherwise accepted as '[rhabdodontids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhabdodontidae)', despite previous records having suggested the contrary.

  [^cite_note-Maidment2026-61]

- Reconstruction of the nasal soft tissues of [ceratopsids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceratopsidae) is presented by Tada et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-294]

- Theurer et al. (2026) provide new information on the structure of the ceratopsid lower leg and feet on the basis of the study of an indeterminate ceratopsid hindlimb from the Upper Cretaceous Dinosaur Park Formation (Alberta, Canada).

  [^cite_note-295]

- A pathological caudal vertebra of *[Medusaceratops lokii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medusaceratops)* affected by chronic osteomyelitis with a draining sinus tract is described from the Upper Cretaceous [Judith River Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judith_River_Formation) (Montana, United States) by Wang et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-296]

- Ames, Rowe & Button (2026) compare biomechanical performance of skulls and horns of five ceratopsian taxa in simulated combat behaviors, and argue that evolution of the diversity of ceratopsian cranial morphologies might have been driven by the diversity of their combat styles.

  [^cite_note-297]
