### Avian research

- Benito et al. (2026) contest the conclusions of the study of Wilken et al. (2025)

  [^cite_note-320]

  &#x20;about the evolution of the ability of birds to move parts of the skull independently, arguing that these conclusions were based on inadequate taxon sampling and morphological misinterpretations;

  [^cite_note-321]

  &#x20;in response Wilken et al. (2026) agree that the bone interpreted in the 2025 study as a coracoid of *[Janavis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janavis)* is more likely to be a pterygoid, but question the affinities of this bone among Mesozoic birds, and overall reaffirm their original conclusion that powered prokinesis is most likely an [autapomorphy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autapomorphy) of [neognath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neognathae) birds.

  [^cite_note-322]

- Review of research from the preceding years on the phylogenetic relationships of birds and on their diversification throughout their evolutionary history is published by Benito& Steell (2026).

  [^cite_note-323]

- Lechki & Benson (2026) compare the reproductive output of extant and extinct amniotes, and link the increase of size of eggs and offspring (relative to adult size) in birds compared to other dinosaurs to the evolution of larger brain, likely also related to increased parental care.

  [^cite_note-324]

- Liu et al. (2026) identify the best predictors of body mass in extant birds, and provide new estimates of body mass of Mesozoic birds.

  [^cite_note-325]

- Jo et al. (2026) report the first discovery of Mesozoic [avialan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avialae)-type eggs from Korea, discovered in the mid-Cretaceous strata of the Ilseongsan Formation (South Korea), and name a new ootaxon *[Onggwanoolithus aphaedoensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Onggwanoolithus?action=edit\&redlink=1)*.

  [^cite_note-326]

- [O'Connor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jingmai_O'Connor) & Marugán-Lobón (2026) revise the fossil record of avialans from the Upper Jurassic [Solnhofen Limestones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solnhofen_Limestone) (Germany), consider purported diagnostic characters of *[Ostromia crassipes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ostromia)* and *[Alcmonavis poeschli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcmonavis)* to be equivocal, and interpret the studied fossils as most likely representing a single taxon (*[Archaeopteryx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeopteryx)*);

  [^cite_note-327]

  &#x20;another revision of Solnhofen avialans is subsequently published by Foth & Rauhut (2026), who interpret the holotype of *Ostromia crassipes* as an indeterminate avialan, and interpret *Alcmonavis poeschli* as likely to be a distinct taxon.

  [^cite_note-328]

- Evidence indicating that *Archaeopteryx* was capable of achieving take-off velocity sufficient to become airborne in two to three leaps is presented by Meilak et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-329]

- Zhou et al. (2026) interpret the evolution of teeth of Mesozoic birds as random and mainly influenced by phylogeny and evolution time, with no evidence of a consistent trend towards tooth loss.

  [^cite_note-330]

- Rivera-Sylva, Guzmán-Gutiérrez & Rangel-Morelos (2026) review the fossil record of Cretaceous birds from Mexico.

  [^cite_note-331]

- Wu et al. (2026) study the evolution of the triosseal canal of birds on the basis of data from *[Archaeorhynchus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeorhynchus)* and a new enantiornithean specimen from the Jiufotang Formation (Liaoning, China), interpreting the triosseal canal as resulting from evolution of the coracoscapular joint into [synchondrosis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synchondrosis) in members of [Ornithothoraces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ornithothoraces), and from subsequent closing of the canal in members of [Ornithuromorpha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ornithuromorpha) as a result of the appearance of the acrocoracoclavicular joint between their coracoid and furcula.

  [^cite_note-332]

- Kuo et al. (2026) report the discovery of an enantiornithean specimen from the Jiufotang Formation with distinctly shaped [uncinate processes of ribs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uncinate_processes_of_ribs) and study the morphological diversity of the uncinate processes of birds, reporting evidence of a link between uncinate process length and ecological attributes of birds, including their flight performance.

  [^cite_note-333]

- Holdaway et al. (2026) study the variation within the moa genus *[Euryapteryx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euryapteryx)*, and find that their phylogenetic relationships indicated by the study of ancient mitochondrial DNA do not match patterns recovered in morphometric analysis.

  [^cite_note-334]

- A study on the hindlimb morphology of extant rheas, and a taxonomic revision of fossil and archaeological rheid material, is published by Picasso, Acosta Hospitaleche & Quaggia (2026).

  [^cite_note-335]

- Mayr et al. (2026) describe new [ergilornithid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ergilornithidae) fossil material from the Miocene locality Kayaca (Beyağaç Basin, Turkey), corroborating [struthioniform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Struthioniformes) affinities of the studied group.

  [^cite_note-336]

- Evidence from the study of ostrich eggshells from Middle Pleistocene to late Holocene sites in Israel, indicative of shift from eggshells with pore patterns similar to those seen in extant [Somali ostrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somali_ostrich) to eggshells with pore patterns similar to those seen in [Arabian ostrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabian_ostrich) which might related to a species turnover in the studied area between 100,000 and 70,000 years ago, is presented by Tsahar et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-337]

- Niespolo et al. (2026) determine the youngest known eggshells of *[Genyornis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genyornis)* to be approximately 46-44,000 years old.

  [^cite_note-338]

- Sosa & Acosta Hospitaleche (2026) describe a synsacrum of an indeterminate member of Neognathae from the Ypresian strata of the [La Meseta Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Meseta_Formation) from the [Seymour Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seymour_Island), interpreted as evidence of presence of a small shorebird or marine bird in Antarctica during the Eocene.

  [^cite_note-339]

- Somogyi et al. (2026) interpret cases of apparent morphological [convergence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convergent_evolution) in members of major lineages of [Neoaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoaves) that lack consistent functional drivers as likely linked to high levels of [incomplete lineage sorting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incomplete_lineage_sorting) during the early radiation of the group in the aftermath of the Cretaceous–Paleogene extinction event, resulting in difficulty of phylogenetic placement of early Cenozoic members of the group.

  [^cite_note-340]

- Hellyer-Price, Venditti & Humphries (2026) calculate the drag on the bill of *[Pelagornis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pelagornis)* while skimming, and argue that the studied bird was likely unable to skim-feed.

  [^cite_note-341]

- Goedert et al. (2026) report the discovery of a braincase of *Pelagornis orri* from the Miocene [Astoria Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astoria_Formation) (Oregon, United States), providing new information on the anatomy of the species, as well as discovery of isolated vertebra and phalanx bone of *Pelagornis* sp. from the Eocene [Keasey Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keasey_Formation) (Oregon), representing one of the oldest pelagornithid records reported to date.

  [^cite_note-342]

- Degrange et al. (2026) study the anatomy of the brain endocast of *[Conflicto antarcticus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conflicto_antarcticus)*, reporting the absence of [Wulsts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperpallium), lack of adaptations to filter-feeding seen in extant [anatids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anatidae), and presence of large olfactory bulbs.

  [^cite_note-343]

- Van der Meer & Langeveld (2026) report the discovery of fossil material of the [harlequin duck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harlequin_duck) from the Quaternary strata from the Dutch [North Sea](21179.md), representing the second fossil record of the species from Europe reported to date.

  [^cite_note-344]

- Citron et al. (2026) study the anatomy of skulls and endocasts of the [dodo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dodo) and the [Rodrigues solitaire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodrigues_solitaire), and report evidence of differences in the visual and olfactory systems of the dodo compared to those of extant doves and pigeons.

  [^cite_note-345]

- Zelenkov (2026) describes fossil material of Pleistocene bustards from the Taurida Cave (Crimea), confirms the validity and distinctiveness of *[Otis lambrechti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Otis_lambrechti?action=edit\&redlink=1)* and *"Otis" kalmani*, and assigns the latter species to the genus *[Tetrax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tetrax)*.

  [^cite_note-346]

- De los Reyes, Acosta Hospitaleche & Sosa (2026) report the discovery of a tarsometatarsus of the [grey-cowled wood rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grey-cowled_wood_rail) from the strata of the La Esperanza Formation (Buenos Aires Province, Argentina), interpreted as suggestive of presence of seasonal wetlands and/or humid scrublands in the studied area during the early Pleistocene.

  [^cite_note-347]

- Lenser, Reed & Worthy (2026) interpret the fossil record of shorebirds from the [Blanche Cave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blanche_Cave) (South Australia) as indicative of mostly terrestrial environment in the studied area in the Pleistocene, including open forest and woodland but also with wetland elements, and interpret changes of composition of the studied assemblage as indicative of decrease in available wetlands at the end of the Last Glacial Maximum.

  [^cite_note-348]

- Evidence of distinct cranial anantomy, sexual size dimorphism and adaptions of hindlimbs to underwater locomotion in the [spectacled cormorant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spectacled_cormorant) is presented by Samsonov et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-349]

- A study on the phylogenetic relationships of the spectacled cormorant as indicated by ancient DNA data is published by Sharko et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-350]

- Xia, Wu & Li (2026) interpret elemental distribution in fossils of Eocene penguins from the [La Meseta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Meseta_Formation) and Submeseta formations (Seymour Island, Antarctica) as providing evidence of changes of weathering intensity, depositional energy and redox conditions in Antarctica related to shift from a warm climate during early Eocene to subsequent cooling during middle-late Eocene.

  [^cite_note-351]

- Probable evidence of sex-related variation in the microstructure of penguin bones from the La Meseta and Submeseta formations is presented by Garat, Acosta Hospitaleche & Talevi (2026).

  [^cite_note-352]

- Mayr & Richter (2026) describe new fossil material of *[Hassiavis laticauda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hassiavis?action=edit\&redlink=1)* from the Eocene [Messel Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Messel_Formation) (Germany), providing new information on the anatomy of members of this species, and reevaluate the phylogenetic affinities of [Archaeotrogonidae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeotrogonidae).

  [^cite_note-353]

- New fossil material of the [Cuban pauraque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuban_pauraque) and the [Antillean nighthawk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antillean_nighthawk) is described from the Pleistocene and Holocene strata from the El Abrón Cave (Cuba) by Gorbatcheva & Zelenkov (2026).

  [^cite_note-354]

- Hunt, [Lucas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spencer_G._Lucas) & Smith (2026) describe [bromalites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bromalite) from the Eocene [Willwood Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willwood_Formation) (Wyoming, United States) interpreted as owl regurgitalites containing bones of glyptosaurid lizards, and name a new ichnotaxon *[Sauresus osteodermus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sauresus?action=edit\&redlink=1)*.

  [^cite_note-355]

- New fossil material of *[Athene vallgornerensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athene_vallgornerensis)*, confirming the taxonomic validity of this species and providing new information on its anatomy, is described from the Pleistocene (Calabrian) strata from Crimea by Zelenkov (2026).

  [^cite_note-356]

- Fossil material of members of two [honeyguide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honeyguide) species, representing the earliest record of members of this group reported to date, is described from the Pliocene strata of the [Varswater Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varswater_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) from the Langebaanweg site (South Africa) by Louchart, Manegold & Pavia (2026).

  [^cite_note-357]

- A study on the bone histology of *[Andrewsornis abbotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrewsornis)* and *[Physornis fortis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physornis)*, providing evidence of uninterrupted growth strategy in [phorusrhacids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phorusrhacidae), is published by Dreyer, Cooper & O'Connor (2026).

  [^cite_note-358]

- Lo Coco et al. (2026) reinterpret *Pleistoanser bravardi* as a parrot belonging to the genus *[Cyanoliseus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyanoliseus)*, resulting in a new combination *[Cyanoliseus bravardi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyanoliseus_bravardi?action=edit\&redlink=1)*.

  [^cite_note-359]

- Mayr (2026) studies the phylogenetic relationships of *[Parapsittacopes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parapsittacopes)* and *[Psittacomimus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psittacomimus)*, assigning them to the new family [Psittacomimidae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psittacomimidae?action=edit\&redlink=1) interpreted as likely [sister group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sister_group) of Parapasseres (the clade formed by the [Zygodactylidae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zygodactylidae) and [Passeriformes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passeriformes)).

  [^cite_note-360]

- A study on the phylogenetic relationships of the [Hawaiian honeyeaters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohoidae), indicative of a relationship with the clade including the families [Hypocoliidae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypocoliidae) and [Hylocitreidae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hylocitreidae) rather than a sister relationship with Hypocoliidae alone, is published by Zhao, Kimball & Braun (2026).

  [^cite_note-361]

- Campana et al. (2026) study the phylogenetic relationships of extant and extinct [Hawaiian honeycreepers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawaiian_honeycreeper), and report evidence of adaptive radiation the group following the formation of [Oʻahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O%CA%BBahu), and find that the [ʻiʻiwi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%CA%BBi%CA%BBiwi) is not closely related to extinct members of the genus *[Drepanis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drepanis)*.

  [^cite_note-362]

- A study on the morphological diversity of Cretaceous to Pleistocene avian tracks from continents that were parts of [Gondwana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gondwana) is published by Farina et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-363]

- Review of the composition of the Eocene bird assemblage from the Walton Member of the [London Clay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_Clay) (United Kingdom) is published by Mayr (2026).

  [^cite_note-364]

- Mayr & Collinson (2026) revise the fossil record of stomach contents of Eocene birds from the [Messel Pit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Messel_Pit) (Germany), reporting evidence of ingested seeds in representatives of ten species belonging to different bird groups, and interpret the studied fossil record as indicative of Eocene frugivorous birds exploiting existing diversity of flowering plants with edible fruits (whose origin predated those of the studied birds) rather than indicative of coevolution of flowering plants and early frugivorous birds.

  [^cite_note-365]

- Farina, Krapovickas & Marsicano (2026) study the composition of the bird track assemblage from the Miocene [Vinchina Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vinchina_Formation?action=edit\&redlink=1) (Argentina), including the oldest rheiform track in southern South America reported to date and probable phorusrhacid track.

  [^cite_note-366]

- Serrano et al. (2026) describe Early Pleistocene bird remains from the Venta Micena site (Spain), interpreted as indicative of presence of wetland environment.

  [^cite_note-367]

- Zelenkov et al. (2026) report the discovery of fossil material of a new late Pleistocene bird fauna from the Khondu locality (Sakha Republic, Russia), including at least 25 taxa.

  [^cite_note-368]

- Oros Sršen et al. (2026) study the composition of the late Pleistocene bird assemblages from the Marlera site (Croatia), reporting evidence of presence of taxa associated by open and dry environments, as well as aquatic taxa.

  [^cite_note-369]

- A study on the composition of the late Pleistocene bird assemblage from the Khondu locality (Sakha Republic, Russia) is published by Maslintsyna & Zelenkov (2026).

  [^cite_note-370]

- A new, diverse Late Pleistocene bird assemblage is reported from the Taguatagua 3 site in the Tagua Tagua Lake area (Chile) by Alarcón-Muñoz et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-371]

- Evidence from the study of ancient environmental DNA indicative of changes of composition of bird communities in high-latitude areas of Eurasia and Alaska from the [Last Glacial Maximum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Last_Glacial_Maximum) to the Holocene is presented by Sander et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-372]

- Peralta et al. (2026) report the discovery of a new avian assemblage from the early Holocene strata from the Molino Doll locality (Entre Ríos Province, Argentina).

  [^cite_note-373]

- A study on the composition on the middle Holocene avian assemblage from the Cueva del Llano site (Fuerteventura, Canary Islands), providing evidence of presence of taxa typical of forest environments and the edges of bodies of water, is published by Sánchez-Marco, Sánchez-Sastre & Castillo (2026).

  [^cite_note-374]

## Pterosaurs

### New pterosaur taxa

| Name | Novelty | Status | Authors | Age | Type locality | Country | Notes | Images |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| *[Laueropterus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laueropterus)*[^cite_note-Laueropterus-375] | Gen. et sp. nov | Valid | Hone | Late Jurassic | [Mörnsheim Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%B6rnsheim_Formation) | [Germany](11867.md) | An early member of [Monofenestrata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monofenestrata). The type species is *L. vitriolus*. |  |
| *[Rexarthuria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rexarthuria)*[^cite_note-376] | Gen. et comb. nov |  | Thomas & McDavid | Early Cretaceous (Valanginian) | [Tunbridge Wells Sand Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tunbridge_Wells_Sand_Formation) | [United Kingdom](31717.md) | An [azhdarchoid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azhdarchoidea); a new genus for "*Palaeornis*' *cliftii* Mantell (1844). |  |
| *[Tylodorhynchus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tylodorhynchus)*[^cite_note-377] | Gen. et comb. nov | Valid | Pêgas & Holgado | Early Cretaceous (Barremian) | [Wessex Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wessex_Formation) | [United Kingdom](31717.md) | A coloborhynchine [anhanguerid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anhanguerid); a new genus for "*[Uktenadactylus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uktenadactylus)*" *rodriguesae* Holgado & Pêgas (2020). |  |

### Pterosaur research

- Walters, [Rayfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emily_Rayfield) & [Donoghue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philip_Donoghue) (2026) argue that modern reconstructions of pterosaur wings likely underestimate the diversity of wing shapes within this group.

  [^cite_note-378]

- Ji (2026) describes a partial skeleton of an indeterminate pterosaur, marking the first record of the group from the Mazongshan Dinosaur Fauna ([Xiagou Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xiagou_Formation), China).

  [^cite_note-J26-379]

- Cerqueira et al. (2026) compare the allometry in the skeletal elements of *[Rhamphorhynchus muensteri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhamphorhynchus)* and pterodactyloid pterosaurs, reporting evidence of differences interpreted as likely linked to different flight capabilities and to differing ecology.

  [^cite_note-380]

- Tong et al. (2026) report the discovery of two specimens of *[Cascocauda rong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cascocauda)* from the Jurassic [Tiaojishan Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiaojishan_Formation) (China) providing new information on the anatomy of [anurognathids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anurognathidae), including evidence of presence of a skull element resembling the supraorbital (or palpebral) bones found in extant [lepidosaurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lepidosauria) and possibly helping to secure the pterosaurs' eyes within the orbits.

  [^cite_note-381]

- A probable [pterodactyloid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pterodactyloidea) [radius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radius_\(bone\)) is reported from the [Bathonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bathonian) strata from the [Stonesfield Slate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stonesfield_Slate) locality (United Kingdom) by Averianov & Lopatin (2026).

  [^cite_note-382]

- Grice et al. (2026) report evidence of preservation of molecular biomarkers in a pterosaur wing phalanx from the Lower Cretaceous [Romualdo Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romualdo_Formation) (Brazil), and interpret the results of molecular analyses of steroids from the studied fossil as consistent with a diet including fish and/or cephalopods.

  [^cite_note-383]

- Unwin et al. (2026) argue that the [holotype](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holotype) of purported pterosaur *[Bakiribu waridza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bakiribu)* is actually fossil material of an indeterminate ray-finned fish (possibly an amiid).

  [^cite_note-384]

- Averianov & Gubarev (2026) report the discovery of a pterosaur cervical vertebra from the Cenomanian [Melovatka Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melovatka_Formation) ([Saratov Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saratov_Oblast), Russia) with similarities to a probable targaryendraconid vertebra from the Albian [Toolebuc Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toolebuc_Formation) (Australia), and interpret the studied vertebrae as likely to be the first diagnostic postcranial remains of members of [Targaryendraconia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Targaryendraconia).

  [^cite_note-385]

- A wing [phalanx bone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phalanx_bone) of a member of [Ornithocheiriformes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ornithocheiriformes) that is the first known pterosaur bone from Egypt is described from the Cenomanian [Bahariya Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahariya_Formation) by Salem et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-386]

- Wang et al. (2026) describe an articulated forelimb of *[Hamipterus tianshanensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamipterus)* from the Lower Cretaceous strata in Xinjiang (China), providing new information on the anatomy of members of this species.

  [^cite_note-387]

- Araújo et al. (2026) use the data from the study of bone histology of *[Caiuajara dobruskii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caiuajara)* to calculate its resting and maximum metabolic rates, recovered as intermediate between those of modern [ectothermic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ectotherm) and [endothermic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endotherm) amniotes, and interpret *C. dobruskii* as likely capable of prolonged powered flight.

  [^cite_note-388]

- Jung et al. (2026) describe track of a large pterosaur (possibly a member of [Neoazhdarchia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoazhdarchia)) from the Lower Cretaceous (Albian) strata of the [Jinju Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jinju_Formation) (South Korea), found in close association with a small tetrapod trackway and possibly documenting predation of a pterosaur on a small tetrapod, and name a new ichnotaxon *[Jinjuichnus procerus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jinjuichnus)*.

  [^cite_note-389]

## Other archosaurs

### Other new archosaur taxa

| Name | Novelty | Status | Authors | Age | Type locality | Country | Notes | Images |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |

### Other archosaur research

- McDavid, Marchant, & Reid (2026) revise the nomenclature of *[Alickmeron maleriensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alickmeron)* and consider it a junior objective synonym of *[Alwalkeria maleriensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alwalkeria)* due to being based on the same holotype and type species, also reinterpreting it as an indeterminate member of [Pan-Aves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-Aves).

  [^cite_note-390]

- Description of the braincase and cranial endocast of the [holotype](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holotype) of *[Venetoraptor gassenae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venetoraptor)* is published by Damke et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-391]

- A femur of one of the largest [silesaurids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silesauridae) known worldwide is described from the [Ladinian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ladinian) strata of the Pinheiros-Chiniquá Sequence of the [Santa Maria Supersequence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Maria_Formation) (Brazil) by Müller (2026).

  [^cite_note-392]

## General research

- Sookias et al. (2026) study the diversification of Permian to Early Jurassic Archosauromorpha, reporting evidence of initial high diversification that decreased through time in archosauromorph subgroups (including archosaur ones), but with different trajectories of this pattern in the studied subgroups.

  [^cite_note-393]

- Napoli (2026) reevaluates two methods (geometric morphometrics and cladistic analysis of ontogeny) used in the studies of extinct vertebrates (especially dinosaurs) aiming to determine which differences between fossil specimens result from variation between different species and which differences result from variation between specimens representing different growth stages (possibly of the same species), reports that application of these methods to extant American and Chinese alligators does not consistently result in correct specific identification, and identifies invariant characters that provide consistent identification of members of different alligator species throughout their life history.

  [^cite_note-394]

- Lechki & Benson (2026) report evidence of uneven effectiveness of energetic fitness models at predicting body size distributions in different amniote groups, with good results in the study of birds but not in the study of crocodylians and non-avian dinosaurs, and interpret the body size distribution of dinosaurs (including lack of non-avian dinosaurs with body mass of less than 300 g) as influenced not only by their physiology but also by ecological constraints (possibly by competition with mammals and with juveniles of large dinosaurs excluding adult dinosaurs from small body sizes), which were lifted in the bird lineage around the time of the evolution of flight.

  [^cite_note-395]

- Seymour et al. (2026) determine blood flow rates of 81 species of extinct [archosauriforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archosauriformes) on the basis of the study of nutrient foramina in their long bones, interpreted as consistent with widespread [tachymetabolism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warm-blooded#Terminology) in Mesozoic archosaurs.

  [^cite_note-396]

- Liard, Liard & [Buffetaut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89ric_Buffetaut) (2026) review the fossil record of tracks of vertebrates (mostly archosaurs) from the Mesozoic strata from Thailand.

  [^cite_note-397]

- Evidence supporting the presence of extraoral tissues ("lips") similar to those seen in extant lepidosaurs in Triassic pseudosuchians and dinosaurs from southern Brazil is presented by Terras et al. (2026), who interpret the presence of "lips" in the form of labial scales associated with extensive [gingiva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gingiva) covering the teeth as likely to be the [plesiomorphic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plesiomorphy_and_symplesiomorphy) condition in [Sauropsida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sauropsida).

  [^cite_note-398]

- Marchetti et al. (2026) study the composition of the tetrapod (mostly archosaur) track assemblage from the [Carnian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnian) strata from the Lerici site (Italy), identifying pseudosuchian, theropod and sauropodomorph tracks and providing evidence of co-occurrence of two different sauropodomorph ichnogenera.

  [^cite_note-399]

- García-Cobeña et al. (2026) report the discovery of new fossil material of vertebrates, including crocodylomorphs and dinosaurs, from the Lower Cretaceous [El Castellar Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Castellar_Formation) (Spain), expanding known vertebrate diversity from the studied formation.

  [^cite_note-400]

- A cluster of bones of an ornithuromorph bird (possibly *[Gansus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gansus)*), interpreted as most likely to be originally contained within a [pellet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pellet_\(ornithology\)) produced by a non-avian theropod (possibly *Jian changmaensis*), is described from the Lower Cretaceous strata of the Xiagou Formation (China) by Wang et al. (2026).

  [^cite_note-401]

- Egan et al. (2026) reconstruct the ecology and environment of the Late Cretaceous (Campanian) ecosystem of the [Dinosaur Park Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dinosaur_Park_Formation) (Saskatchewan, Canada) on the basis of the study of isotopic composition of tooth enamel of vertebrates (mostly archosaurs) and amber, interpreting the studied archosaur assemblage as living close to the coastline of the [Western Interior Seaway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Interior_Seaway), and finding no evidence of significant differences in dietary and migratory behaviors between hadrosaurs and ceratopsians.

  [^cite_note-402]

- Fanti et al. (2026) identify the Late Cretaceous (Campanian) fossil assemblage (including ornithopod *[Tethyshadros insularis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tethyshadros)* and crocodyliform *[Acynodon adriaticus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acynodon)*) from the Villaggio del Pescatore site (Italy) as living in a tidal flat ecosystem repeatedly affected by tropical cyclones and flood events.

  [^cite_note-403]

- Soto-Acuña et al. (2026) describe fossil material of large-bodied ornithopods from the Maastrichtian strata of the Quebrada Municipalidad Beds (Chile), and identify purported Eocene [presbyornithid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presbyornithid) remains from the same locality as actually originating from the Upper Cretaceous strata.

  [^cite_note-404]

- A [coprolite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coprolite) produced by a medium-sized theropod (possibly *[Nanotyrannus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanotyrannus)* or *[Anzu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anzu_wyliei)*), preserving skeletal remains of a [hesperornithiform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hesperornithes) bird and feathers that likely belonged to the same bird, is described from the Maastrichtian strata of the Hell Creek Formation (Montana, United States) by O'Connor et al. (2026), who interpret the morphology of the studied feathers as indicative of adaptations of hesperornithiform feathers for reduced penetrability similar to those observed in extant aquatic birds, and indicative of presence of both anatomically modern lightweight and stiff feathers and extinct feather morphotypes in hesperornithiforms.

  [^cite_note-405]

- Hack et al. (2026) report the discovery of two isolated [unguals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unguals) from the Late Triassic (Rhaetian) [Exter Formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exter_Formation) (Germany), interpreted as belonging to a sauropodomorph and potentially the largest Triassic pterosaur.

  [^cite_note-406]
